On Tuesday, Luis actually brought in leftover tilapia. Fish! In a warehouse breakroom with zero windows. It smelled so horrendous that Marcus started gagging over by the water cooler, and Brenda had to prop the double doors open with these heavy wooden pallets just to air the room out. \n\nBut she left the pallets sticking out into the main forklift lane, so Dave—he's the safety manager—he came down and completely lost his mind. Dave has this really high-pitched voice when he gets mad, almost like a squeaky toy? He was yelling at Brenda and waving his clipboard around, and the clip snapped, so all his safety checklists went flying all over the concrete floor. It took three of us to help him pick them up, and the papers got all covered in that black warehouse dust. He was so mad he made Luis take his fish out to the dumpster in the parking lot. \n\nSo yeah, I really don't know the answer to your question. I just haven't tried it to see what would happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to restrict your food intake if you couldn't see your body?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know how to answer that either. If I couldn't see my body? Like, if I was blind or something? I guess I haven't ever really thought about that. I'm not sure if I'd want to restrict or not. \n\nBut talking about not being able to see things... the lighting in the warehouse is just terrible. Half the fluorescent bulbs in aisle four have been burned out for like three weeks, and they just flicker constantly. It gives me this awful headache right behind my eyes. I was trying to pack this order of novelty mugs on Thursday—and honestly, who buys a mug shaped like a toilet? It's so gross. He slammed on the brakes, and the whole stack of cardboard boxes he was moving tipped over. Just... hundreds of flat, unassembled boxes sliding all over the dusty floor. We spent half my shift just picking them up and trying to restack them so they wouldn't slide again. \n\nSo yeah, I really don't know what I'd do if I couldn't see my body. I just haven't ever thought about a situation like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your desire to control your eating is more about not gaining weight or about feeling in control of your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don't know... She even made Marcus check inside the breakroom trash can, which is just disgusting because someone had thrown away half a burrito in there earlier. \n\nAnd then it turns out the sticky note was stuck to the bottom of her own shoe the whole time! It echoed through the whole building. \n\nSo yeah, I really couldn't tell you if it's more about weight or control. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I mean, I do get really scared about that. Like, if I eat something heavy... say, fried food from the break room, or like a big plate of food at church, I just get terrified it's going to show on me right away. I literally can't stop thinking about it, and I just want to go find a scale immediately to check. 

But oh my gosh, speaking of the fried food in the break room... you wouldn't believe what Dave tried to serve us on Friday for the "employee appreciation" lunch. He ordered from this cheap chicken place down the street, but he didn't want to pay the delivery fee, so he made Marcus go pick it up in his old Honda Civic. By the time Marcus got back, the grease had completely soaked through the cardboard boxes and ruined his passenger seat. He was so mad. 

And the chicken was just completely cold and soggy by the time they set it up. But Brenda was still trying to hype it up, standing by the microwave with her little plastic tongs, acting like it was this gourmet meal. She even made this big banner out of printer paper that said "You're a HEAD of the flock!" with a clip-art picture of a chicken head. It was so weird. And the smell of that old grease just lingered in the break room all afternoon because the exhaust fan above the microwave has been broken since November. It just hung in the air all the way down aisle four while I was packing boxes. 

So, yeah... if I eat stuff like that, I just panic and need to check the scale. It just really freaks me out.

He went to the bathroom to wash it off, but our soap dispenser has been empty for two weeks, so he just had to wipe his hands on his jeans. He looked like he'd been fingerpainting. And then when Dave saw the purple glitter on the official time log, he started squeaking again about professional documentation and threatened to make everyone clock in at the main office across the lot, which would add like ten minutes to our walk. \n\nSo, yeah. I literally can't stop thinking about it, and I just want to go find a scale immediately to check. \n\nBut oh my gosh, speaking of the fried food in the break room... you wouldn't believe what Dave tried to serve us on Friday for the \"employee appreciation\" lunch. He ordered from this cheap chicken place down the street, but he didn't want to pay the delivery fee, so he made Marcus go pick it up in his old Honda Civic. By the time Marcus got back, the grease had completely soaked through the cardboard boxes and ruined his passenger seat. He was so mad. \n\nAnd the chicken was just completely cold and soggy by the time they set it up. She even made this big banner out of printer paper that said \"You're a HEAD of the flock!\" with a clip-art picture of a chicken head. It was so weird. And the smell of that old grease just lingered in the break room all afternoon because the exhaust fan above the microwave has been broken since November. It just hung in the air all the way down aisle four while I was packing boxes. \n\nSo, yeah... if I eat stuff like that, I just panic and need to check the scale. It just really freaks me out."
